NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
 
THIS AGREEMENT  is made and entered into between the Navajo County Public Health 
Services District (the “District”), a political subdivision of the State of Arizona, and Debra 
Smalley, RNP, CNM (the “Subcontractor”). 
 
WHEREAS, the District requires the services of a consultant nurse practitioner to assist in the 
fulfillment of the District’s contracts with the Arizona Department of Health Services (ADHS) for 
Family Planning Services, STD Services, HIV Services, and Immunization Services.  Services 
are also required for billable services as the provider of record  for diagnosis, treatment and 
prescribing. 
 
WHEREAS, the Subcontractor is a nurse practitioner licensed to practice in Arizona; and 
 
WHEREAS, the District wishes to contract with the Subcontractor for reproductive health, STI, 
HIV & immunizations consulting and physical examination services as described herein, and the 
Subcontractor is willing to provide such services upon the terms and conditions set forth herein, 
 
NOW, THEREFORE , the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Term. This Agreement shall commence as of 1 January 2020 (the “Effective Date”) and 
shall renew annually on January 1, unless requests to amend or terminate are submitted 
in writing by either party at least 60 days prior to the January 1 renewal date. 
 
2. Services. The Subcontractor shall provide various health consulting and physical 
examination services and shall sign routine standing orders and review medical 
protocols related to immunizations, family planning, STI, and HIV; all in accordance with 
the ADHS Contract s, and under the general direction of the Medical Director of the 
District.  The Subcontractor acknowledges receipt of a copy of the ADHS Contracts and 
understands and agrees that all services hereunder shall be provided in strict 
compliance with the ADHS Contracts. All services shall be provided in accordance with 
the professional and ethical standards applicable to Registered Nurses and Certi fied 
Nurse Practitioners, and within the contractor’s legal scope of practice. 
  
 The Subcontractor shall act as the provider named in various contracts with Medicare, 
Medicaid, and Private Insurances for billable public health services relevant to 
immunizations, family planning, STD, and HIV.  The Subcontractor shall work with 
various insurance, billing, and laboratory subcontractors of Navajo County Public Health 
Services District to ensure appropriate credentialing is in place, and up-to-date.

<!-- page 570 -->
Services for reproductive health shall be provided two and a half days per month  (two 
full days at the district’s Show Low office, and  one-half day in the district’s Holbrook 
office).  Consultation and physical examination for immunization, STI & HIV services will 
be available on an as needed basis during these hours, and remotely during other times 
throughout the month. The parties shall agree upon a mutually satisfactory schedule , 
which can be adjusted by either party as needed. 
 
3. Compensation. As full and complete com pensation for the services to be provided 
hereunder, the District shall pay to the Sub contractor a monthly fee of $ 3,000 ($2,000 
per month from the Family Planning grant , and $1,000 per month from the health 
district). 
 
Family Planning three days at the Show Low Office    $ 1,750.00 
Family Planning one half day at the Holbrook Office    $    500.00 
Laboratory, STD, HIV & Immunization Orders & Insurance   $ 1,000.00 
 
                          
                                                                     Totaling  $3,250.00 
 
At the beginning of the first month that this Agreement is in effect , and at the beginning 
of each month thereafter, the contractor will invoice Navajo County Public Health for the 
hours worked.  The Public Health Nursing Supervisor  will submit to Navajo County a 
demand in the amount of the monthly fee.  Payment shall be made directly to the 
Subcontractor, in accordance with the County’s standard procedures for processing 
demands. 
 
4. Independent Contractor.   In providing services hereunder, the Subcontractor is an 
independent contractor. The Subcontractor shall not be deemed an employee of the 
District and shall not be entitled to any benefits provided to the District Employees.  
Taxes, Social Security and other amounts customarily with held from the earnings of 
employees shall not be withheld from the compensation paid to the Subcontractor.  
 
5. Insurance.   The Subcontractor shall maintain in force during the term of this 
Agreement, at the Subcontractor’s expense, professional liability in surance, in the 
amount of no less than $1,000,000 per occurrence , and such other insurance as the 
District’s Risk Manager may reasonably require. The Subcontractor shall provide the 
District with certificates of insurance evidencing all required policies a nd shall notify the 
District of any cancellation or decrease in the amount of coverage at least 30 days 
before the effective date of such cancellation or decrease. 
 
6. Compliance with Laws.    The Subcontractor shall comply with all federal and state 
statutes, regulations and orders applicable to the services provided hereunder. All 
federal and state laws required to be incorporated into the Agreement shall be enforced 
as though fully set forth herein.  The Subcontractor will work with in their professional 
scope of practice and be expected to understand what those limitations are. 
 
7. Professional Licenses.    The Subcontractor shall maintain in force throughout the 
term of this Agreement any and all licenses, permits and accreditations required for the 
Subcontractor to provide services hereunder.  The Subcontractor shall notify the District 
and shall immediately cease performance hereunder if any such license, permit or

<!-- page 571 -->
accreditation is suspended or revoked.  These licenses must include at minimum a valid 
RN or NP license, current CPR certification, current fingerprint clearance and current 
PPD skin test. 
 
8. Reports and Records.    The record on a client at the time of physical examination 
and all other client records shall be retained in the District Office.  The Subcontractor is 
familiar with all state, and department record-retention and confidentiality requirements 
set forth in the ADHS Contract and applicable federal and state laws and shall strictly 
comply with all such requirements in handling client records and information. 
 
9. Indemnification.   To the fullest extent allowed by law, each party (as “Indemnitor”) 
shall indemnify, defend and hold harmless the other party and its agents, 
representatives, officers, officials and employees (the “Indemnitees”) from and ag ainst 
any and all claims, damages, losses and expenses (including but not limited to attorney 
fees, court costs and the costs of appellate proceedings) relating to, arising out of or 
resulting from the Indemnitor’s negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omiss ions in the 
performance of this Agreement.  The Indemnitor’s negligent acts, errors, mistakes or 
omissions in the performance of this Agreement.  The Indemnitor’s duty to defend, hold 
harmless and indemnify the Indemnitees shall arise in connection with an y claim, 
damage, loss or expense that is attributable to bodily injury, sickness, disease, death or 
injury to any person, or impairment or destruction of property including loss of use 
resulting therefrom, caused in whole or in part by any negligent act, e rror, mistake or 
omission in the performance of this Agreement (including those by any person for whose 
negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions the Indemnitor may be liable). 
 
10. Termination.   This Agreement shall terminate immediately and automatically upon the 
termination of the ADHS Contract for any reason.  In addition, either party may terminate 
this Agreement for a ny reason upon 6 0 days prior written notice to the other party.  In 
addition, the District may terminate this Agreement upon written no tice if the 
Subcontractor fails to cure any default in performance within ten days after delivery of a 
written notice of default by the District. This Agreement is also subject to cancellation 
pursuant to ARS §§ 38-511 (concerning conflicts of interest). 
 
11. Non-Assignment.   The Subcontractor shall not assign any right or interest in this 
Agreement without the District’s prior written approval, nor shall the Subcontractor 
delegate or subcontract any duty hereunder without the District’s prior written approval. 
Any purported assignment, delegation or subcontract without the District’s prior written 
approval shall be void.

Board of Supervisors Regular 5. a.        
Meeting Date: 01/14/2020  
Title: FY20 Arizona Governor's STOP Grant
Submitted For: Brad Carlyon, County Attorney  Submitted By: Lynda Young, Office
Manager
Department: County Attorney
Presented By: Lynda Young/Roxanne Pergeson
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and acceptance of the FY2020 Arizona Governor's STOP Grant
#IGA-ST-19-010118-01Y2. 
Background:
This is the 2nd year renewal grant for the Governor's Office STOP grant.  Funds from this grant
pay for the Sexual Assault Response Team Coordinator.  This SART Coordinator recruits, trains,
and coordinates multi-disciplines to respond to adult victims of sexual assault.  This year's funds
will also pay for training for the SART team as well as SART protocol training.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval and acceptance of the 2nd year STOP Grant
#IGA-ST-19-010118-01Y2. 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY19-20
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $51,360
Fiscal Impact:
75% funded through Arizona Governor's STOP grant and 25% funded through County Attorney
General Fund.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6. a.        
Meeting Date: 01/14/2020  
Title: Resolution Designating Public Roads Within the TASRD#1 as Primitive
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 04-2020, approving/denying
designating public roads within the Timberland Acres Special Road District #1 as primitive roads.
Background:
The Timberland Acres Special Road District #1 was established by the Navajo County Board of
Supervisors in September, 1978. Funding for the road district comes from Timberland Acres
property owners through the Navajo County property taxation system. The Timberland Acres
road system is comprised of approximately 11.5 miles of roads, about half of which are paved
with chip seal.  The road district is well managed, and Navajo County has supported their
maintenance efforts throughout the years.  Designating the roads within the district's geographic
boundaries as primitive will allow the County to continue partnering with the district on road
projects, and assure compliance with current state statutes addressing primitive roads and
maintenance of public roads and streets.        
 
Recommendation: 
Approve the resolution designating the roads within the TASRD#1 geographic boundaries as
primitive.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2019-20
Budgeted Y/N: N/A
Amount Requested: N/A
Fiscal Impact:
No fiscal impact at this time

RESOLUTION ____-20 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS TO 
DESIGNATE PUBLIC ROADS WITHIN THE TIMBERLAND ACRES SPECIAL ROAD 
DISTRICT #1 AS PRIMITIVE ROADS  
 
 
WHEREAS Pursuant to A.R.S. 28-6706, the Board of Supervisors may designate a public 
road(s) within the Navajo County Maintained Road System as a primitive road if it was opened 
before June 13, 1990 and was not constructed in accordance with County standards and is not a 
County Highway; and  
 
WHEREAS, the public roads within the Timberland Acres Special Road District #1 (TASRD#1), 
identified on Exhibit 1 hereto, meet the criteria specified in  A.R.S. 28-6706; and  
 
WHEREAS, the TASRD#1 Board of Trustees  agree that the primitive road designation for the 
roads within their geographic boundaries is in the best interests  of their residents and ongoing 
maintenance program; and   
 
WHEREAS, The TASRD#1 is responsible for all maintenance, repair and snow removal 
operations on public roads within its geographic boundaries as identified in Exhibit 1; and 
 
WHEREAS, a designated primitive road is not regularly maintained by the County , and that 
neither the County nor its employees are liable for damages or injuries resulting from the use of a 
primitive road e xcept for intentional injuries or gro ss negligence caused by an employee acting 
within the scope of the employee’s employment; and  
 
WHEREAS, The County may undertake routine or non -routine work on the primitive roads 
within the District's boundaries in compliance with A.R.S. 28 -6705 when it is jointly determined 
by the County's Public W orks Director or designee and the Chair of the Board of Trustees of the 
District to be in the best interest of the County, or to protect the health and safety of Navajo 
County residents. 
  
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF 
SUPERVISORS AS FOLLOWS:  
 
• The Board r ecognizes the importance of partnerships and lever aging road maintenance 
resources when practicable and allowed by law.  
• The Board  may designate a public road(s) within the Navajo  County Maintained Road

<!-- page 600 -->
System as a primitive road if it was opened before June 13, 1990 and was not const ructed 
in accordance with County standards and is not a County Highway. 
• The Boar d recognizes it is in the best inter est of the County and the resident s of the 
Timberland Acres Road District #1  to designate the specified roads with in the district as 
primitive roads.  
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Navajo County Board of Superviso rs on this 
___ day of ___________, 2020.  
 
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
 By ________________________________________ 
                                                                            Jason E. Whiting, Chairman of the Board

Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Boar d

Board of Supervisors Regular 6. b.        
Meeting Date: 01/14/2020  
Title: IGA Between Navajo County and Timberland Acres Special Road District #1
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of the Intergovernmental Agreement between Navajo County and Timberland
Acres Special Road District #1 regarding road maintenance.
Background:
The Timberland Acres Special Road District #1 (TASRD #1) was formed by the Navajo County
Board of Supervisors in September 1978.  The County has partnered with the district on its road
maintenance efforts throughout the years.  The purpose of the proposed IGA is to facilitate
continuing road maintenance partnership opportunities between TASRD#1 and Navajo County,
within the framework of both the County's Maintained Road System Policy and A.R.S. statutes
pertaining to primitive roads and the maintenance of public roads and streets.   
Recommendation: 
Approve the IGA between the Timberland Acres Special Road District #1 and Navajo County.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6. c.        
Meeting Date: 01/14/2020  
Title: BIA/County Operators
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Miles Begay, Engineering Tribal Trans Manager
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Approving Task Agreement No. 19 to Memorandum of Agreement between the
Bureau of Indian Affairs Department of Transportation- Navajo Region and Navajo County for
the continued funding support of two Navajo County Road Maintenance Equipment Operators. 
Background:
The County and the Bureau of Indian Affairs Department of Transportation had a successful
operational partnership this year as it relates to our joint agreement where the County provides the
funding for and hiring of heavy equipment operators to the Navajo Nation within Districts I and
II. The operators continue to significantly assist BIA with road maintenance within the Navajo
Nation communities, especially the local routes that have been neglected for some time now. This
new agreement will continue to support the current (2) equipment operators in providing road
maintenance/ blading work on BIA- Navajo Region inventory roads through December 31, 2020. 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends that the Board of Supervisors approve the attached Task Agreement between
BIA DOT and Navajo County. 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2020
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 60,000
Fiscal Impact:
$30,000 from District 1 Special Road Funds.
$30,000 from District 2 Special Road Funds
 
Fiscal Year: 2021
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 60,000

Task Agreement NO. 19 
Fort Defiance and Chinle Agency- Division  of Transportation 
Navajo County Heavy Equipment Operators 
The 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors, by motion duly passed at the Board’s regular meeting 
on January 14, 2020, has approved the expenditure of up to $120,000.00 in (X) Special Project 
Road Funds, or ( ) Highway User Revenue Funds to be used for the funding of two Navajo County 
Road Maintenance Equipment Operators, for road maintenance activities on BIA public roads, 
which are located on the Navajo Indian Reservation within BIA Chinle and Fort Defiance 
Agency, in Navajo County. The funds shall be used to support the road maintenance crew and 
fuel purchase that are or may become available to the Bureau for road maintenance purposes on 
the BIA public roads in that portion of the Navajo Reservation, within Navajo County’s 
Supervisorial District No. 1 and 2. Such duties may include, but are not limited, to: operating a 
motor grader, backhoe, end loader, or truck; installing road signs; mowing vegetation; repairing 
cattle guards and guardrails; cleaning, repairing, or installing drainage structures; repairing r
 i ght - 
of- wa y fences; patching and crack sealing pavement, placing gravel to alleviate a safety hazard 
when directed by BIA supervisor; and performing similar road maintenance activities. 
The BIA warrants and  represents that the employee(s) funded and the fuel purchased with the 
Allocated Funds will be used primarily for maintenance activities on roads within District 1 and 2 
of Navajo County. BIA understands that Navajo County will be responsible for hiring and 
paying the Equipment Operators. The applicants will undergo hiring process by Navajo County’s 
criteria an
d be chosen by the reviewers’ determination. The County will determine the salary and 
benefits that the two employee(s) will be receiving. Once the employee(s) are hired, the BIA will 
provide maintenance activities whenever employee(s) are working on BIA public roads. 
Navajo County will be responsible for all personnel issues in accordance with the County 
personnel policies. Navajo County will provide a motor grader to be utilized while BIA will 
furnish any additional equipment needed for the employee(s).  The County will be responsible 
for routine maintenance checkups on the motor grader it provides.  The employee(s)  shall be 
utilized until December 31, 2020 and then shall no longer be available for road 
maintenance activities pursuant to this Agreement.
